George van Egmond, Engelken Tols, Wouter van Bijler, Jacobus Fabius, Hillegoent van Alendorp, Sigismund II Augustus, Bona Sforza, Catherine Jagiellon, Isabella Jagiellon, John Sigismund Zapolya, Elizabeth I, William Parr, Elisabeth Brooke, William Herbert, Anne Heneage, Thomas Heneage, Thomas Stanley, Richard Martin, Dorcas Ecclestone, Edmund Withypoll, Maria Dimock, John Dymock, Michel de Castelnau and Jonekin van Herwijck are in this study's network and are NOT offered here: sitters, patrons and family, not makers, or makers with no evidenced standalone record in this run -- judgement exclusions by this study, not carried exclusions. Richard Martin is in this study's network and is NOT offered here: a maker (goldsmith, Master of the Mint) whose record is civic and mint-administrative, and whose portrait medallion is the evidence for his birth date rather than part of a body of artistic work -- judgement exclusion by this study, not a carried exclusion. Giovanni Giacomo Caraglio and Hans Schwarz are in this study's network and are NOT offered here: parallel medallists of the same Polish sitters, but their records in this leg are single-medal attributions, too thin to carry as candidates without further research -- judgement exclusions by this study, not carried exclusions. Hans Reinhart the Elder is in this study's network and is NOT offered here: fully verified as a spare (see network entry 39) but held back because the German medallic line is a step sideways from Herwijck's Antwerp-London axis; use if any of the 11 proposed fails -- judgement exclusion by this study, not a carried exclusion. ## Corrections

- C529. The doorway tally. This is the one-hundred-and-sixty-fourth doorway check in a row; the hold tally counts only since #420, so the streak now stands at one hundred and seventeen holds and forty non-holds. The #542 study file's tally (116 holds, 39 non-holds, 163 checks) was carried forward correctly.
- C530. The Paris travelling-party error in the #542 top-pick paragraph. The paragraph says Vinkeles "went to Paris in 1770 with Jacques-Philippe Le Bas, travelling with Jurriaan Andriessen, Izaak Schmidt and Hermanus Numan, and met Izaak Jansz de Wit (1744-1809)". The sources do not support that grouping: the Brabant trip with Andriessen and Schmidt was 1765; in Paris he studied under Le Bas and met Numan and de Wit there. He travelled to Paris alone as far as any source says. The study corrects the grouping in the life section and records the doorway D3 as holding in substance and failing in the letter. The task brief for this run carried the same conflation; the brief is not a source.
- C531. The "founder of Pax Artium Nutrix" error. The #542 top-pick paragraph calls Vinkeles "a founder of Pax Artium Nutrix". Leg 1 (F6) and leg 3 (section 1, FLAG) both report that every source found says member only -- EN Wikipedia, NL Wikipedia via RKD -- and two targeted searches found no founder list and no founding date for the club. The study does not call him founder anywhere; the doorway D5 is recorded as failing in substance and in letter.
- C532. The whole-log mentions. The #542 top-pick paragraph claims "zero whole-log mentions" for Vinkeles. The parent's grep finds three mentions, all in the #542 log entry itself: the Depth line (leg 3 read EN Wikipedia Reinier Vinkeles end to end), the Push line (study/jan-punt.md push note naming the #543 top pick), and the Future-candidates line (top pick for #543). All three are carries, not studies; the substance (never studied before) holds, the letter (zero mentions) fails. Same class as #542 C519.
- C533. The "New from #543" count and the Marcus promotion. The #541/#542 convention delivers 15 new names (4 mains + 1 reserve + 2 alternates + 8 spares). This run delivers 11 new names (4 mains + 1 reserve + 2 alternates + 4 spares) because only 11 genuinely new names came out of Vinkeles's network: Jacobus Buys is already carried as a #541 main and Cornelis Bogerts is already carried as a #542 extra verified spare, so neither counts as new. Jacob Ernst Marcus is promoted from the #542 extra verified spares paragraph -- where he was held back because no dates were established -- after leg 3 of this run found 1774-1826; he is proposed as main 5. The carried #542 extra spares paragraph still contains his held-back entry verbatim; it is a historical record of #542's proposal, superseded by the new main entry here.
- C534. The RISD line-count discrepancy. The RISD Felix Meritis Drawing Academy page is 55 lines as counted by leg 1 and 101 lines as counted by leg 2 -- same object (2008.16.3), different line counts. The counts are counting-rule or render differences, not different pages; the study cites both. Same class as #542 C524.
- C535. Leg 1's Q22. Q22 is a quote BY Jacob Haafner ("The discovery of India by the Europeans is the biggest disaster this country has been able to face"), not by Vinkeles. Leg 1 labels it CONTEXT itself; the study keeps it as context for the Haafner plates (W20) and does not count it among Vinkeles's own words. Q1-Q8 are the only words BY Vinkeles in this study.
- C536. Leg 2's summary dimension counts. Leg 2's handoff summary says "5 VERIFIED ... 8 NOT VERIFIED", but the report's own entries count 4 VERIFIED in W1-W20 (W3, W17, W18, W19) plus 2 more VERIFIED in Beyond the 20 (Tuileries, Noyades), for 6 verified dimensions in total, and 9 NOT VERIFIED in W1-W20 (W6, W9, W10, W11, W12, W13, W14, W16, W20), not 8. The study follows the entries (4 + 7 + 9 = 20; 6 verified counting Beyond the 20). The summary's two counts are each one short of the entries.
- C537. The birth-date spread. Leg 1 (F1): Wikidata-derived infoboxes give 12 January 1741; the article bodies of NL/FR/RU Wikipedia, Knoef (1933) and the scholarly BWN (Jung) give 19 June 1741. The study follows BWN (19 June) and carries the date as 19 June 1741 in the Site line, with the dispute recorded here and in the Disputes section. The 12 January date's origin was not established in this leg.
- C538. The Felix Meritis date spread. Leg 1 (F4) and leg 2 (flag 2): Knoef dates the two hall prints 1792 and 1794; BWN says "omstreeks 1801"; the Met dates its impression "ca. 1800"; RISD dates its impression 1801. The study carries W19 as 1801 per RISD, with the spread noted in the work entry and the Disputes section. Unresolved whether Knoef's dates refer to the drawings, or there are two campaigns, or one source is wrong.
- C539. The Do NOT re-pick list, carried verbatim without silent alteration. The list sits in the carried Future candidates chain (from the #522 block) and ends at Francois Dumont (#505); Vinkeles (#543) does not appear in it and was not added. The list is a record of what was excluded at the time it was written, not a live index.
- C540. Leg 3's Picart line count. The EN Wikipedia Bernard Picart article is 208 lines as counted by leg 3 of this run and 209 lines as counted by leg 3 of #542. Same page, different line counts -- a counting-rule difference (highest displayed line number + 1 vs "of N" total), same class as #542 C524. The study cites both.
